(69h)

Thus, each Kasner worldsheet creation and annihilation operator is expressed as a mixture of the right- and left-moving global mode operators
\gamma
. However, these
\gamma
oscillators correspond to the Unruh-Minkowski basis, which is defined to be valid only in the F and P wedges. We aim to adjust the relations in (
69
) so that they translate into the Bogoliubov transformations between the Kasner worldsheet operators and their inertial partners in the Minkowski basis. To achieve this, we take an arbitrary linear combination of all the obtained right- and left-moving Kasner worldsheet operators, labeling them as
\tilde{\beta}_{n}^{\mu}\sim c_{1}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-8.86531pt{\rm F}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-8.86531pt{\rm F}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-7.95142pt{\rm F}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-7.95142pt{\rm F}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}+c_{2}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-9.0014pt{\rm P}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-9.0014pt{\rm P}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-8.04863pt{\rm P}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-8.04863pt{\rm P}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}
and
{\beta}_{n}^{\mu}\sim c_{1}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-7.84415pt{\rm F}\kern 4.53441pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-4.14552pt\kern 4.53441pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-7.84415pt{\rm F}\kern 4.53441pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-4.14552pt\kern 4.53441pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-5.1674pt{\rm F}\kern 2.77156pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-2.38268pt\kern 2.77156pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-4.37553pt{\rm F}\kern 1.97969pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-1.5908pt\kern 1.97969pt{n}}}}+c_{2}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-7.98024pt{\rm P}\kern 4.53441pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-4.14552pt\kern 4.53441pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-7.98024pt{\rm P}\kern 4.53441pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-4.14552pt\kern 4.53441pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-5.26462pt{\rm P}\kern 2.77156pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-2.38268pt\kern 2.77156pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-4.47275pt{\rm P}\kern 1.97969pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-1.5908pt\kern 1.97969pt{n}}}}
. By appropriately fixing the constants
(c_{1},c_{2})
, we can choose a basis that ensures each pair of
\gamma
operators in the F and P wedges aligns precisely with the original Minkowski worldsheet operators
\big\{\tilde{\alpha}_{n}^{\mu},{\alpha}_{n}^{\mu}\big\}
. This adjustment leads to the desired Bogoliubov transformations between the Kasner and Minkowski worldsheet operators that are valid throughout the spacetime, including both F and P wedges, as follows:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}

\displaystyle={1\over\sqrt{2\sinh\left(\pi\Omega_{n}\over a\right)}}\left[e^{\pi\Omega_{n}\over 2a}\,\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}-e^{-{\pi\Omega_{n}\over 2a}}\,\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}\right],

(70a)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}

\displaystyle={1\over\sqrt{2\sinh\left(\pi\Omega_{n}\over a\right)}}\left[e^{\pi\Omega_{n}\over 2a}\,\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}-e^{-{\pi\Omega_{n}\over 2a}}\,\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}\right],

(70b)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}

\displaystyle={1\over\sqrt{2\sinh\left(\pi\Omega_{n}\over a\right)}}\left[e^{\pi\Omega_{n}\over 2a}\,\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}-e^{-{\pi\Omega_{n}\over 2a}}\,\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}\right],

(70c)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}

\displaystyle={1\over\sqrt{2\sinh\left(\pi\Omega_{n}\over a\right)}}\left[e^{\pi\Omega_{n}\over 2a}\,\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}-e^{-{\pi\Omega_{n}\over 2a}}\,\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}\right].

(70d)

The above mappings are analogous to the Bogoliubov transformations between Minkowski and Rindler worldsheet operators
Bagchi:2020ats
;
Bagchi:2021ban
. These new Bogoliubov transformations, as expressed in (
70
), will be crucial in our exploration of the tensionless limit on the Kasner worldsheet from the viewpoint of an observer situated on the Minkowski worldsheet (see
section

4
).

3.5
The evolving worldsheet vacua

Now we would like to explore the Kasner worldsheet vacuum state
\lvert 0_{\rm K}\rangle
, as defined by the
\beta
operators in (
49
), and establish its mapping with the Minkowski worldsheet vacuum
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
of
\alpha
operators. From the perspective of an observer on the Minkowski worldsheet, the
\beta
operators are always connected to
\alpha
operators via the Bogoliubov transformations (
70
). This naturally introduces the notion of a vacuum state
\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle
and associated operators
\big\{\tilde{\beta}_{n}^{\mu}(a),{\beta}_{n}^{\mu}(a)\big\}
for the Kasner worldsheet that evolve continuously with the time-evolution parameter
a
. This is defined by:

\displaystyle\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle:~\mathchoice{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}(a)\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle=\mathchoice{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}(a)\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle=0,\kern 5.0pt\forall~n>0,

(71)

followed by the Bogoliubov transformations (see
eq.

70
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathchoice{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}(a)=\beta_{+}(a)\,\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}+\beta_{-}(a)\,\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}},\\[6.0pt]
\mathchoice{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}(a)=\beta_{+}(a)\,\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}+\beta_{-}(a)\,\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}},\end{split}

(72)

where the Bogoliubov coefficients are defined as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\beta_{+}(a)&={e^{\pi\Omega_{n}\over 2a}\over\sqrt{2\sinh\left(\pi\Omega_{n}\over a\right)}}={1\over\sqrt{1-e^{-{2\pi\Omega_{n}\over a}}}},\\[10.0pt]
\beta_{-}(a)&=-{e^{-{\pi\Omega_{n}\over 2a}}\over\sqrt{2\sinh\left(\pi\Omega_{n}\over a\right)}}=-{e^{-{\pi\Omega_{n}\over a}}\over\sqrt{1-e^{-{2\pi\Omega_{n}\over a}}}}.\end{split}

(73)

We now aim to establish a mapping between the Minkowski worldsheet vacuum
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
and the evolving vacuum states
\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle
. Following the standard procedure (e.g., see
Smaldone:2018oyl
;
Katagiri:2023wul
;
Das:1997gg
), we identify
\beta_{+}(a)=\cosh\theta_{n}
and
\beta_{-}(a)=\sinh\theta_{n}
, such that

\displaystyle\tanh\theta_{n}(a)={\beta_{-}(a)\over\beta_{+}(a)}=-e^{-{\pi\Omega_{n}\over a}}.

(74)

We then define a generator for the Bogoliubov transformations (
72
) in the typical form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}(\theta_{n})=-i\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\theta_{n}(a)\left[\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{n}}}}\cdot\mathchoice{{\alpha}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{n}}}}-\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}\cdot\mathchoice{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}\right],

(75)

followed by a unitary operator (also recognized as the squeezing operator) given by,

\displaystyle U(\theta_{n})=e^{i\mathcal{Q}(\theta_{n})}.

(76)

With the above setup, the relations (
73
) for the evolving operators can be recast into the Bogoliubov–Valatin transformations:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathchoice{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}(a)=U(\theta_{n})^{\dagger}\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}\,U(\theta_{n})=\cosh\theta_{n}\,\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}+\sinh\theta_{n}\,\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}},\\[6.0pt]
\mathchoice{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}(a)=U(\theta_{n})^{\dagger}\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}\,U(\theta_{n})=\sinh\theta_{n}\,\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}+\cosh\theta_{n}\,\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}.\end{split}

(77)

These forms provide an alternative expression for the same Kasner worldsheet operators as derived in
eq.

70
. Consequently, the evolving vacuum states satisfy the following condition for all
n>0
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathchoice{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}(a)\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle=\left(\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}+\tanh\theta_{n}\,\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}\right)\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle=0,\\[6.0pt]
\mathchoice{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}(a)\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle=\left(\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}+\tanh\theta_{n}\,\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}\right)\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle=0.\end{split}

(78)

The unitary operator
U(\theta_{n})
then connects the evolving vacua
\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle
to the Minkowski worldsheet vacuum
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
in the standard manner, which is given by:

\displaystyle\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle=U(\theta_{n})\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\theta_{n}(a)\left[\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}\cdot\mathchoice{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}-\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{n}}}}\cdot\mathchoice{{\alpha}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{n}}}}\right]\right)\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle

\displaystyle=\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}{1\over\cosh\theta_{n}}\exp\left(-{\tanh\theta_{n}\over n}\,\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}\cdot\mathchoice{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}\right)\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle,

(79)

where the last step is obtained with the help of standard Gaussian decomposition treatment and the
\alpha
operator commutation relation (
12
). Notably, flipping the positions of the holomorphic and anti-holomorphic oscillators does not alter the typical form of
\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle
. Finally, substituting the relations (
73
) for the
\theta_{n}
parameter in terms of
a
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle=\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{C}_{n}\exp\left({e^{-{\pi\Omega_{n}\over a}}\over n}\,\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}\cdot\mathchoice{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}\right)\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle,

(80)

where
\mathcal{C}_{n}=\sqrt{1-e^{-2\pi\Omega_{n}/a}}
. Therefore, from the perspective of the observer on the Minkowski worldsheet, the new vacua
\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle
of the Kasner worldsheet is obtained as a ‘coherent’ or ‘squeezed’ state in terms of the Minkowski worldsheet vacuum
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
and its oscillators
\big\{\tilde{\alpha}_{n}^{\mu},{\alpha}_{n}^{\mu}\big\}
. This evolving vacua is hence a highly energized state with respect to the inertial one
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
. Notably, the relations (
78
) and (
3.5
) between
\big\{\mathchoice{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}(a),\mathchoice{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}(a)\big\}
and
\big\{\tilde{\alpha}_{n}^{\mu},{\alpha}_{n}^{\mu}\big\}
are also invertible, yielding:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle=\left(\mathchoice{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}(a)-\tanh\theta_{n}\,\mathchoice{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}(a)\right)\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle=0,\\[6.0pt]
\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle=\left(\mathchoice{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}(a)-\tanh\theta_{n}\,\mathchoice{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}(a)\right)\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle=0.\end{split}

(81)

Consequently, we can express
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
as a squeezed state of
\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle
, given by:

\displaystyle\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle=\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{C}_{n}\exp\left(-{e^{-{\pi\Omega_{n}\over a}}\over n}\,\mathchoice{\tilde{\beta}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}_{{{-n}}}}(a)\cdot\mathchoice{{\beta}_{{{-n}}}}{{\beta}_{{{-n}}}}{{\beta}_{{{-n}}}}{{\beta}_{{{-n}}}}(a)\right)\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle,

(82)

which is an inverted perspective of (
80
). This is precisely the worldsheet equivalent of the Kasner observer perceiving the Minkowski counterpart and its associated vacuum
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
, which is well-aligned with the timelike Unruh effect discussed in
section

5.2.2
.

4
Tensionless limit on Kasner worldsheets

In this section, we aim to embed the exact notion of “tensionless-ness” on the closed string Kasner worldsheet evolving with a scaled time-evolution parameter
a
, as depicted in
section

3
. To date, no intrinsic framework exists for tensionless Kasner worldsheets, similar to the framework available for the null or Carrollian Minkowski worldsheet in
section

2.3
. Naively attempting to start from the inertial stage (
15
) and then applying Unruh’s methodology (as employed in
section

3.4
) for structuring modes on the tensionless Kasner worldsheet would likely fail due to the null or degenerate structure of tensionless strings. Therefore, we must consider a limiting approach to the tensile Kasner worldsheet setup. A successful example of such an approach has been developed in
Bagchi:2020ats
;
Bagchi:2021ban
for tensionless Rindler worldsheets. Adapting this idea, we will explore the limit where the Kasner worldsheet approaches its horizon, specifically the regime around
{a\over c}\to\infty
. This choice of infinite time-evolution limit on Kasner worldsheets is motivated by the role of infinite boost or Carrollian limits in inducing tensionless physics on the Minkowski worldsheet (see
section

2.3
). Specifically, we are ambitious about conducting a possible litmus test: as the tensile Kasner worldsheet approaches its horizon, the Bogoliubov transformations (
70
) should truncate in a manner that precisely replicates the mapping (
20
) between tensionless and tensile worldsheet oscillators.

We would like to mention that the set of Bogoliubov transformations (
70
) between the
\beta
and
\alpha
oscillators of Kasner and Minkowski worldsheets are self-consistent in the sense that their respective commutations never disrupt their typical mappings. By reading off the Kasner worldsheet parameters
\Omega_{n}
,
\mathcal{L}_{\varphi}
and
\varphi
, as defined in
section

3
, we impose the limit
{a\over c}\to\infty
and systematically obtain:

\displaystyle{\Omega_{n}\over a}={2\pi cn\over\ln\left(1\pm{a\ell\over c}e^{a\psi}\right)}\to{2\pi cn\over\left[\ln\left(\pm{a\ell\over c}\right)+a\psi\right]}\approx{2\pi cn\over a\psi}.

(83)

Evidently, the term on the right-hand side acquires a small value in the near-horizon (or infinite Kasner evolution) limit
{a\over c}\to\infty
, causing the transcendental functions within the Bogoliubov coefficients to expand around a small phase factor
{\pi\Omega_{n}\over 2a}
, followed by expressing:

\displaystyle{e^{\pm{\pi\Omega_{n}\over 2a}}\over\sqrt{2\sinh\left(\pi\Omega_{n}\over a\right)}}\approx{1\over 2}\left(\sqrt{2a\over\pi\Omega_{n}}\pm\sqrt{\pi\Omega_{n}\over 2a}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left({\pi\Omega_{n}\over 2a}\right)^{3/2}.

(84)

By implementing these relations, the Bogoliubov transformations (
70
) are successively expanded. At leading order (i.e., neglecting terms of
\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{c^{3}/a^{3}}\right)
and higher), we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-10.5667pt{\infty}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-10.5667pt{\infty}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-9.16669pt{\infty}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-9.16669pt{\infty}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}

\displaystyle={1\over 2}\left[\left(\sqrt{\pi^{2}cn\over a\psi}+\sqrt{a\psi\over\pi^{2}cn}\right)\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}+\left(\sqrt{\pi^{2}cn\over a\psi}-\sqrt{a\psi\over\pi^{2}cn}\right)\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}\right],

(85a)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\beta^{{\kern-9.54553pt{\infty}\kern 4.53441pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-4.14552pt\kern 4.53441pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\beta^{{\kern-9.54553pt{\infty}\kern 4.53441pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-4.14552pt\kern 4.53441pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\beta^{{\kern-6.38268pt{\infty}\kern 2.77156pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-2.38268pt\kern 2.77156pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\beta^{{\kern-5.5908pt{\infty}\kern 1.97969pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-1.5908pt\kern 1.97969pt{n}}}}

\displaystyle={1\over 2}\left[\left(\sqrt{\pi^{2}cn\over a\psi}+\sqrt{a\psi\over\pi^{2}cn}\right)\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}+\left(\sqrt{\pi^{2}cn\over a\psi}-\sqrt{a\psi\over\pi^{2}cn}\right)\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}\right],

(85b)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-10.5667pt{\infty}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-10.5667pt{\infty}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-9.16669pt{\infty}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-9.16669pt{\infty}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}

\displaystyle={1\over 2}\left[\left(\sqrt{\pi^{2}cn\over a\psi}+\sqrt{a\psi\over\pi^{2}cn}\right)\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}+\left(\sqrt{\pi^{2}cn\over a\psi}-\sqrt{a\psi\over\pi^{2}cn}\right)\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}\right],

(85c)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-9.54553pt{\infty}\kern 4.53441pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-4.14552pt\kern 4.53441pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-9.54553pt{\infty}\kern 4.53441pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-4.14552pt\kern 4.53441pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-6.38268pt{\infty}\kern 2.77156pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-2.38268pt\kern 2.77156pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-5.5908pt{\infty}\kern 1.97969pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-1.5908pt\kern 1.97969pt{-n}}}}

\displaystyle={1\over 2}\left[\left(\sqrt{\pi^{2}cn\over a\psi}+\sqrt{a\psi\over\pi^{2}cn}\right)\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}+\left(\sqrt{\pi^{2}cn\over a\psi}-\sqrt{a\psi\over\pi^{2}cn}\right)\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}\right].

(85d)

Evidently, the above mapping obtained in the near-horizon/light-cone limit of the Kasner worldsheet corresponds to the same as the Bogoliubov transformations (
20
) between tensionless and tensile operators of an intrinsic worldsheet. Comparing (
20
) and (
85
) leads us to the identification of the tensionless state of the Kasner worldsheet,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-10.5667pt{\infty}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-10.5667pt{\infty}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-9.16669pt{\infty}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-9.16669pt{\infty}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}},\quad\mathchoice{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\beta^{{\kern-9.54553pt{\infty}\kern 4.53441pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-4.14552pt\kern 4.53441pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\beta^{{\kern-9.54553pt{\infty}\kern 4.53441pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-4.14552pt\kern 4.53441pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\beta^{{\kern-6.38268pt{\infty}\kern 2.77156pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-2.38268pt\kern 2.77156pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\beta^{{\kern-5.5908pt{\infty}\kern 1.97969pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-1.5908pt\kern 1.97969pt{n}}}},\quad\mathchoice{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-10.5667pt{\infty}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-10.5667pt{\infty}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-9.16669pt{\infty}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-9.16669pt{\infty}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}},\quad\mathchoice{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\beta^{{\kern-9.54553pt{\infty}\kern 4.53441pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-4.14552pt\kern 4.53441pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\beta^{{\kern-9.54553pt{\infty}\kern 4.53441pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-4.14552pt\kern 4.53441pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\beta^{{\kern-6.38268pt{\infty}\kern 2.77156pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-2.38268pt\kern 2.77156pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\infty}}}}\beta^{{\kern-5.5908pt{\infty}\kern 1.97969pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-1.5908pt\kern 1.97969pt{-n}}}},

(86)

followed by inferring:

\displaystyle\epsilon={\pi^{2}nc\over\psi a},\quad\forall~n>0.

(87)

Interestingly, the above identifications allow us to estimate the same mode expansion (
15
) for a Kasner worldsheet in the tensionless limit.

4.1
Time evolution and Carrollian limit

Now, let us interpret the scenario above, specifically how the concept of ‘tensionless-ness’ enters within the framework of the Kasner worldsheet. In other words, how reaching the Kasner worldsheet horizons replicates the same physics as a Minkowski worldsheet in the infinite boost limit
\epsilon\to 0
(refer to
section

2.3
). By examining the identification in (
87
), we can argue that reaching the tensionless point or the horizon of the Kasner worldsheets can be achieved through two possible routes (up to constant factors
n
and
\psi
):
8
8

8

Referring to
fig.

1
, the
\eta=\text{const.}
straight lines represent trajectories for a fixed Kasner parameter
a
. Route I analytically traces the constant
\xi
hyperbolas, which degenerate into the light-cone, achieved by increasing
a\to\infty
while keeping
c
constant. In contrast, Route II follows the constant
\eta
straight lines, ultimately collapsing onto the null horizons as
c\to 0
with
a
held fixed.

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{Route\, I:}\quad c=\text{constant},\,a\to\infty\implies\epsilon\to 0,\\[8.0pt]
&\text{Route\, II:}\quad a=\text{constant},\,c\to 0\implies\epsilon\to 0.\end{split}

(88)

These two routes to reach the Kasner worldsheet light-cone horizon represent distinct physical realizations of the
\epsilon
-parametric evolution, culminating in the infinite boost limit
\epsilon\to 0
, as discussed in
section

2.3
. Route II, in particular, can be seen as achieving the infinite boost state by directly applying the Carrollian limit (
19
) on the Kasner worldsheet while keeping the time-evolution parameter
a
constant. In terms of the closed Kasner worldsheet coordinates, this can be algebraically implemented by contracting the time coordinate
\xi
as follows

\displaystyle\eta\to\eta,\quad\xi\to\epsilon\xi,\quad\epsilon\to 0.

(89)

This setup effectively makes
\eta
very large, allowing one to reach the Kasner worldsheet horizon or light-cone. On the other hand, Route I involves an evolution of the Kasner worldsheet in parameter
a
, eventually reaching the infinite limit
a\to\infty
. The Bogoliubov transformations of the related evolving oscillators
\big\{\tilde{\beta}_{n}^{\mu}(a),{\beta}_{n}^{\mu}(a),\tilde{\beta}_{-n}^{\mu}(a),{\beta}_{-n}^{\mu}(a)\big\}
have already been demonstrated in (
72
). Consequently, there exists a ‘flow’ of decreasing worldsheet tension throughout the range
a\in[0,\infty]
as follows

\displaystyle\begin{split}a=0&~~:~~\big\{\tilde{\beta}_{n}^{\mu},{\beta}_{n}^{\mu},\tilde{\beta}_{-n}^{\mu},{\beta}_{-n}^{\mu}\big\}\longrightarrow\big\{\tilde{\alpha}_{n}^{\mu},{\alpha}_{n}^{\mu},\tilde{\alpha}_{-n}^{\mu},{\alpha}_{-n}^{\mu}\big\},\\[9.0pt]
0<a<\infty&~~:~~\big\{\tilde{\beta}_{n}^{\mu}(a),{\beta}_{n}^{\mu}(a),\tilde{\beta}_{-n}^{\mu}(a),{\beta}_{-n}^{\mu}(a)\big\},\\[9.0pt]
a\to\infty&~~:~~\big\{\tilde{\beta}_{n}^{\mu},{\beta}_{n}^{\mu},\tilde{\beta}_{-n}^{\mu},{\beta}_{-n}^{\mu}\big\}\longrightarrow\{\mathchoice{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}},\mathchoice{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}},\mathchoice{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}},\mathchoice{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}\big\}.\end{split}

(90)

Incorporating this flow into the analysis in
section

3.5
, the tensionless vacuum state
\lvert 0_{c}\rangle
is obtained as a special limiting case of the relevant evolving vacua
\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle
, as defined in (
71
), and can be expressed as a highly energized squeezed state in terms of the tensile inertial vacuum
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
:

\displaystyle\lvert 0_{c}\rangle=\lim_{a\to\infty}\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle=\mathcal{N}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\exp\left({1\over n}\,\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}\cdot\mathchoice{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}\right)\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle,

(91)

where
\mathcal{N}
is an (infinite) normalization constant arising after setting
a\to\infty
in (
80
). Notably, the
\epsilon
-parameter evolution also aligns with the framework of (
74
) and (
3.5
), where
\tanh\theta_{n}(\epsilon)={\epsilon-1\over\epsilon+1}
, leading to the same tensionless vacuum in the analogous limit
\epsilon\to 0
. Similarly, using the representation (
82
), we can derive the tensile state
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
as a coherent state of the tensionless vacuum
\lvert 0_{c}\rangle
, expressed as:

\displaystyle\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle=\hat{\mathcal{N}}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\exp\left(-{1\over n}\,\mathchoice{\tilde{c}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{c}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{c}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{c}_{{{-n}}}}\cdot\mathchoice{{c}_{{{-n}}}}{{c}_{{{-n}}}}{{c}_{{{-n}}}}{{c}_{{{-n}}}}\right)\lvert 0_{c}\rangle,

(92)

where
\hat{\mathcal{N}}
is a different normalization constant in the limit
a\to\infty
. Both mappings (
91
) and (
92
) suggest a contrasting yet intriguing picture, which we will further explore in the subsequent section.

5
Conclusions

This section concludes the work by summarizing key findings and discussing their potential implications.

5.1
Summary of results

The implications of the so-called time-like entanglement within string worldsheet theory remain unexplored. In this work, we addressed the crucial question of the fate of a string propagating in a Kasner background spacetime, with a focus on the Carrollian limit that emerges in the intriguing tensionless regime of this time-evolving, non-inertial worldsheet system. Notably, previous studies
Socolovsky:2013rga
;
Olson:2010jy
;
Higuchi:2017gcd
;
Quach:2021vzo
indicate that conventional (R-L) entanglement has a directly analogous interpretation in time-like (F-P) entanglement. This observation motivated us to develop a framework for Kasner worldsheet theory by mirroring the well-established analysis of an accelerated Rindler worldsheet
Bagchi:2020ats
;
Bagchi:2021ban
.

In
section

3
, we began with the standard setup of a tensile, closed string worldsheet
X^{\mu}(\tau,\sigma)
propagating in
D
-dimensional Minkowski background target spacetime. From this perspective, we constructed a new target spacetime covering the F-P regions by employing the typical transformations (
23
), modulated by the Kasner parameter
a
. This led to a reparametrization (
26
) of the worldsheet coordinates
\left(\tau,\sigma\right)
into
\left(\xi(\tau,\sigma),\eta(\tau,\sigma)\right)
, culminating in the formulation of the Kasner worldsheet
\bar{X}^{\mu}(\xi,\eta)
with novel properties, including folding points and revised periodicity.

We found that the folding points, located at
c\tau=\pm\sigma
, arise due to the non-inertial nature of the Kasner worldsheet, governed by the scaled time-evolution parameter
a
. At these points, the induced metric degenerates, and the Kasner worldsheet reaches the infinite time-evolution limit
{a\over c}\to\infty
, corresponding to horizons on the Kasner worldsheet. Originally, the periodicity of the worldsheet diverges. However, through a systematic regularization procedure, we derived a new form of periodicity (
40
) and related mappings (
41
), which we utilized throughout this work. The regularization parameter was carefully tuned to overlap with the infinite time-evolution limit of the Kasner worldsheet horizons.

Interestingly, the Kasner worldsheet’s periodicity is time-like, contrasting with conventional worldsheet theories
Polchinski:1998rq
. We interpreted this by showing that the mapping
X^{\mu}(\tau,\sigma)\to\bar{X}^{\mu}(\xi,\eta)
induces a temporal shift, driven by the Kasner parameter
a
. This shift alters the spatial/temporal nature of the worldsheet and its coordinates, causing a rapid shift in periodicity along
\xi
in the Kasner framework, compared to the
\sigma
direction in its inertial counterpart. In contrast, for a Rindler worldsheet, analogous mappings involve a spatial boost, maintaining the space-like periodicity
Bagchi:2021ban
.

We then examined the quantum regime of a tensile Kasner worldsheet by performing a quantum mode expansion. This involved promoting the Kasner worldsheet modes to their global forms in terms of the Minkowski basis, linking the oscillation operators to their inertial counterparts through Bogoliubov transformations (see
eq.

70
). The Bogoliubov coefficients were found to be exponential functions of the Kasner parameter
a
, resulting in a time-evolving vacuum state, identified as a squeezed/coherent state relative to the Minkowski vacuum.

Finally in
section

4
, we explored the transition to the tensionless regime of the Kasner worldsheet. Two limiting routes were derived:

(i)

a\to\infty
for a constant speed of light
c
, and

(ii)

c\to 0
for a constant time-evolution
a

, which truncate the tensile Kasner worldsheet framework (including the Bogoliubov transformations, quantum modes, oscillators, and vacuum state) and achieve the tensionless limit. These routes are complementary and effectively describe a continuous flow from a tensile state to a tensionless regime across the Kasner evolution range
a\in[0,\infty]
.

In summary, reaching the horizons at the infinite time-evolution limit induces a Carrollian structure on the Kasner worldsheet, thereby recovering its tensionless physics. Similarly, Rindler worldsheets are known to achieve a tensionless limit at infinite acceleration. This correspondence at the tensionless or Carrollian point establishes that time-like (F-P) entanglement in Kasner worldsheets is not just analogous to, but precisely equivalent to, standard (R-L) entanglement in Rindler worldsheets, observed in distinct pairs of causally disconnected regions of the background spacetime. In essence, exploring Kasner worldsheets provides a standalone and robust “time-like window” for probing the tensionless realm of fundamental strings. This insight is anticipated to reveal numerous intriguing aspects of string theory, which we intend to investigate in future work. For example, it would be compelling to study the fate of time-evolving strings as they approach a generic black hole horizon in the tensionless limit.

5.2
Further discussions and implications

In this note, we extend the discussion by thoroughly analyzing the critical implications of tensionless physics and examining the associated limits that shape the Kasner worldsheet framework.

5.2.1
Emergence of open string states

In string theory, open strings are often viewed as defects on the boundaries of closed string worldsheets. The fundamental boundary conditions for open strings can be expressed in terms of closed string boundary states as follows
Blumenhagen:2009zz

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{\tau}X^{\mu}(\sigma,\tau)\big\rvert_{\tau=0}\lvert\mathfrak{B}_{\rm N}\rangle=0&~\qquad\text{Neumann condition},\\[8.0pt]
\partial_{\sigma}X^{\mu}(\sigma,\tau)\big\rvert_{\tau=0}\lvert\mathfrak{B}_{\rm D}\rangle=0&~\qquad\text{Dirichlet condition},\end{split}

(93)

where
\lvert\mathfrak{B}_{\rm N}\rangle
and
\lvert\mathfrak{B}_{\rm D}\rangle
are regarded as Neumann and Dirichlet boundary states, respectively. Using the Minkowski worldsheet mode expansion (
11
), the boundary conditions (
93
) can be expressed in terms of the inertial
\alpha
-oscillators as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(\tilde{\alpha}_{n}^{\mu}+{\alpha}_{-n}^{\mu}\right)\lvert\mathfrak{B}_{\rm N}\rangle=\left({\alpha}_{n}^{\mu}+\tilde{\alpha}_{-n}^{\mu}\right)\lvert\mathfrak{B}_{\rm N}\rangle=0,\\[6.0pt]
\left(\tilde{\alpha}_{n}^{\mu}-{\alpha}_{-n}^{\mu}\right)\lvert\mathfrak{B}_{\rm D}\rangle=\left({\alpha}_{n}^{\mu}-\tilde{\alpha}_{-n}^{\mu}\right)\lvert\mathfrak{B}_{\rm D}\rangle=0,\end{split}

(94)

for each
n>0
. These conditions, which relate the right- and left-moving closed string modes acting on the boundary states, are the so-called
gluing conditions
. In other words, the conditions (
94
) effectively treat the two halves of the string as a pair of open strings by gluing them together. The conditions (
94
) can be solved explicitly by following the procedure used to obtain the squeezed state (
3.5
) respectively for
\tanh\theta_{n}=\pm 1
, yielding:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lvert\mathfrak{B}_{\rm N}\rangle&=\mathcal{N}_{\rm N}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\exp\left(-{1\over n}\,\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}\cdot\mathchoice{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}\right)\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle,\\[6.0pt]
\lvert\mathfrak{B}_{\rm D}\rangle&=\mathcal{N}_{\rm D}\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\exp\left({1\over n}\,\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}\cdot\mathchoice{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}_{{{-n}}}}\right)\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle.\end{split}

(95)

Interestingly, the tensionless worldsheet vacuum state representations in (
91
) and (
92
) can be identified as the typical boundary states depicted in (
95
). If we elaborate further, it becomes evident that the coherent representation (
91
) of the tensionless Kasner vacuum
\lvert 0_{c}\rangle
, expressed in terms of the usual inertial worldsheet vacuum
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
and its
\alpha
-oscillators, forms a Dirichlet boundary state in all spacetime directions. On the other hand, in terms of the
\lvert 0_{c}\rangle
vacuum and its
c
-oscillators, the coherent state form (
92
) of the inertial vacuum
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
corresponds to a Neumann boundary state. This observation indicates that, as the tension vanishes, closed-string worldsheets transition to an open-string description. To validate this phenomenon, we can verify whether these coherent vacuum states
\lvert 0_{c}\rangle
and
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
satisfy the gluing conditions in a form similar to (
94
), thereby confirming their identification as worldsheet boundary states. In terms of the inertial
\alpha
-operators, the evolving Kasner vacua
\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle
(as defined in (
78
)) can be expressed as

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}-e^{-{\pi\Omega_{n}\over a}}\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}\right)\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle=\left(\mathchoice{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}-e^{-{\pi\Omega_{n}\over a}}\mathchoice{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\alpha}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}\right)\lvert 0_{\rm K}(a)\rangle=0.

(96)

Similarly, with respect to the evolving Kasner operators
\beta(a)
, the Minkowski vacuum
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
(as defined in (
81
)) reads,

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}(a)+e^{-{\pi\Omega_{n}\over a}}\mathchoice{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}(a)\right)\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle=\left(\mathchoice{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{n}}}}(a)+e^{-{\pi\Omega_{n}\over a}}\mathchoice{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}(a)\right)\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle=0.

(97)

If we now take the typical tensionless limit
{a}\to\infty
(keeping
c
constant), the evolving Kasner oscillators and associated vacua translate into tensionless
c
-operators and the vacuum state
\lvert 0_{c}\rangle
in (
96
) and (
97
), yielding:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(\tilde{\alpha}_{n}^{\mu}-{\alpha}_{-n}^{\mu}\right)\lvert 0_{c}\rangle&=\left({\alpha}_{n}^{\mu}-\tilde{\alpha}_{-n}^{\mu}\right)\lvert 0_{c}\rangle=0,\\[6.0pt]
\left(\tilde{c}_{n}^{\mu}+{c}_{-n}^{\mu}\right)\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle&=\left({c}_{n}^{\mu}+\tilde{c}_{-n}^{\mu}\right)\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle=0.\end{split}

(98)

These are clearly the gluing conditions that identify the tensionless Kasner vacuum
\lvert 0_{c}\rangle
and the tensile Minkowski vacuum
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
as Dirichlet and Neumann boundary states, respectively. Thus, taking the tensionless limit on any closed string worldsheet results in the emergence of open strings. This situation, where defect states appear on the closed worldsheets, arises when all points collapse onto the null or horizon lines
c\tau=\pm\sigma
. To summarize, from the perspective of an observer in the inertial worldsheet vacuum
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
, the evolving Kasner vacuum at the tensionless limit
\lvert 0_{c}\rangle
is interpreted as a Dirichlet boundary state, representing a spacetime point known as a D-instanton
Polchinski:1998rq
. Conversely, from the viewpoint of the null Kasner vacuum
\lvert 0_{c}\rangle
, the Minkowski vacuum
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
serves as a Neumann boundary state, corresponding to a space-filling D-brane
Bagchi:2019cay
.

Figure 2:
Illustration of a closed string Kasner worldsheet deforming with increasing
a
as seen from the Minkowski worldsheet. In the upper half, the cylindrical snapshot at
a=0
shows the inertial state. As
a
increases (
a_{2}\gg a_{1}
) keeping
c
constant, the worldsheet distorts into hyperboloids with growing eccentricity, eventually transforming into a light-cone at
a\to\infty
. The lower half shows the
\tau=0
cross-sectional snapshots of this evolution.

Figure 3:
Illustration of a closed string on the Minkowski worldsheet deforming as observed from the Kasner worldsheet along changing constant
\eta
lines, as shown in
fig.

1
. The circular snapshot on the left represents the pure tensile state. As the slope of the
\eta
lines decreases or increases (
\eta_{2}\gg\eta_{1}
), the string distorts into an ellipse, ultimately aligning with the light-cone at
\eta\to\pm\infty
.

The above scenario of open string physics emerging from closed strings directly corresponds to the so-called
null string complementarity
, which has already been observed for a Rindler worldsheet
Bagchi:2020ats
;
Bagchi:2021ban
. Similarly, for the current case of the Kasner worldsheet, this idea can be physically interpreted as follows. From the perspective of an observer on the tensile Minkowski worldsheet, the Kasner worldsheet with increasing
a
evolves from a cylinder to distorted hyperboloids (which are a natural generalizations of Kasner particle worldlines), finally transforming into a light-cone in the tensionless limit
a\to\infty
. For the Dirichlet boundary state (
91
), when zooming in on the
\tau=0
cross-section, where the boundary states (
94
) are defined, the evolution appears as a circle with decreasing radius, eventually collapsing to a point, representing the emergence of a D-instanton (see
fig.

2
). Conversely, an observer on the Kasner worldsheet, as
a\to\infty
, will perceive the Minkowski worldsheet as becoming increasingly elongated as its tension decreases, ultimately becoming infinitely stretchable. This interpretation views the Neumann boundary state (
92
) as D-branes progressively filling all of spacetime. This latter viewpoint physically manifests for different observers on the Kasner worldsheet, positioned along constant
\eta
lines from
\eta=0
to
\eta\to\pm\infty
along a fixed
\xi
hyperbola (e.g., see
fig.

3
), as they witness the complete sequence of deformation from a tensile to a tensionless Minkowski worldsheet. Thus, as the closed string worldsheet becomes null or tensionless, both Kasner and Minkowski observers gain complementary perspectives on the emergence of open string physics.

5.2.2
Time-like Unruh effect on tensionless Kasner worldsheets

The observer on the Kasner worldsheet will see particles defined by the
\beta
operators in the Minkowski worldsheet vacuum
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
. In this context, we can define the following number operators for the
\beta
particles as

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\tilde{N}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{N}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{N}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{N}_{{{n}}}}={1\over n}\mathchoice{\tilde{\beta}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}_{{{-n}}}}\cdot\mathchoice{\tilde{\beta}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{\beta}_{{{n}}}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lc}{1\over n}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-8.86531pt{\rm F}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-8.86531pt{\rm F}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-7.95142pt{\rm F}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-7.95142pt{\rm F}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}\cdot\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-8.86531pt{\rm F}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-8.86531pt{\rm F}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-7.95142pt{\rm F}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-7.95142pt{\rm F}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}\tilde{N}^{{\kern-8.86531pt{\rm F}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}\tilde{N}^{{\kern-8.86531pt{\rm F}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}\tilde{N}^{{\kern-7.95142pt{\rm F}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}\tilde{N}^{{\kern-7.95142pt{\rm F}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}&~:\ \rm F\\
\\
{1\over n}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-9.0014pt{\rm P}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-9.0014pt{\rm P}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-8.04863pt{\rm P}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-8.04863pt{\rm P}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}\cdot\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-9.0014pt{\rm P}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-9.0014pt{\rm P}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-8.04863pt{\rm P}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}\tilde{\beta}^{{\kern-8.04863pt{\rm P}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}\tilde{N}^{{\kern-9.0014pt{\rm P}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}\tilde{N}^{{\kern-9.0014pt{\rm P}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}\tilde{N}^{{\kern-8.04863pt{\rm P}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm P}}}}\tilde{N}^{{\kern-8.04863pt{\rm P}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}&~:\ \rm P\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{N}_{{{n}}}}{{N}_{{{n}}}}{{N}_{{{n}}}}{{N}_{{{n}}}}={1\over n}\mathchoice{{\beta}_{{{-n}}}}{{\beta}_{{{-n}}}}{{\beta}_{{{-n}}}}{{\beta}_{{{-n}}}}\cdot\mathchoice{{\beta}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}_{{{n}}}}{{\beta}_{{{n}}}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lc}{1\over n}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-7.84415pt{\rm F}\kern 4.53441pt}}_{{\kern-4.14552pt\kern 4.53441pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-7.84415pt{\rm F}\kern 4.53441pt}}_{{\kern-4.14552pt\kern 4.53441pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-5.1674pt{\rm F}\kern 2.77156pt}}_{{\kern-2.38268pt\kern 2.77156pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-4.37553pt{\rm F}\kern 1.97969pt}}_{{\kern-1.5908pt\kern 1.97969pt{-n}}}}\cdot\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-7.84415pt{\rm F}\kern 4.53441pt}}_{{\kern-4.14552pt\kern 4.53441pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-7.84415pt{\rm F}\kern 4.53441pt}}_{{\kern-4.14552pt\kern 4.53441pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-5.1674pt{\rm F}\kern 2.77156pt}}_{{\kern-2.38268pt\kern 2.77156pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}{\beta}^{{\kern-4.37553pt{\rm F}\kern 1.97969pt}}_{{\kern-1.5908pt\kern 1.97969pt{n}}}}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}{N}^{{\kern-10.39447pt{\rm F}\kern 7.08473pt}}_{{\kern-6.69585pt\kern 7.08473pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}{N}^{{\kern-10.39447pt{\rm F}\kern 7.08473pt}}_{{\kern-6.69585pt\kern 7.08473pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}{N}^{{\kern-6.86708pt{\rm F}\kern 4.47124pt}}_{{\kern-4.08235pt\kern 4.47124pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm F}}}}{N}^{{\kern-5.58958pt{\rm F}\kern 3.19374pt}}_{{\kern-2.80486pt\kern 3.19374pt{n}}}}&~:\ \rm F\\

where all spacetime indices are contracted. The expectation values of the above-listed number operators in state
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
describe the average number of particles detected by the Kasner worldsheet observer in the Minkowski worldsheet vacuum. For an example, the expected number of particles of frequency
\Omega_{n}
associated with
\mathchoice{\tilde{N}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{N}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{N}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{N}_{{{n}}}}
operator is given by,

\displaystyle\langle 0_{\rm M}\lvert\mathchoice{\tilde{N}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{N}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{N}_{{{n}}}}{\tilde{N}_{{{n}}}}\rvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}=\rm F,P}{1\over{2\sinh\left(\pi\Omega_{n}\over a\right)}}\langle 0_{\rm M}\left|{1\over n}e^{-{\pi\Omega_{n}\over a}}\,\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm\Lambda}}}}\tilde{\gamma}^{{\kern-9.06946pt{\rm\Lambda}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm\Lambda}}}}\tilde{\gamma}^{{\kern-9.06946pt{\rm\Lambda}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm\Lambda}}}}\tilde{\gamma}^{{\kern-8.09724pt{\rm\Lambda}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm\Lambda}}}}\tilde{\gamma}^{{\kern-8.09724pt{\rm\Lambda}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{-n}}}}\cdot\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}}}}\tilde{\gamma}^{{\kern-10.53195pt{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}}}}\tilde{\gamma}^{{\kern-10.53195pt{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}}}}\tilde{\gamma}^{{\kern-9.55974pt{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}}}}\tilde{\gamma}^{{\kern-9.55974pt{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\kern 5.55557pt}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}\right|0_{\rm M}\rangle

\displaystyle={e^{-{\pi\Omega_{n}\over a}}\over{2\sinh\left(\pi\Omega_{n}\over a\right)}}\,\delta_{0,0}={{1}\over{e^{\Omega_{n}\over a/2\pi}-1}}\,\delta_{0,0}.

(104)

Similarly, for the case of
\mathchoice{{N}_{{{n}}}}{{N}_{{{n}}}}{{N}_{{{n}}}}{{N}_{{{n}}}}
operator, we can have

\displaystyle\langle 0_{\rm M}\lvert\mathchoice{{N}_{{{n}}}}{{N}_{{{n}}}}{{N}_{{{n}}}}{{N}_{{{n}}}}\rvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle={{1}\over{e^{\Omega_{n}\over a/2\pi}-1}}\,\delta_{0,0}.

(105)

Note that the infinite factor
\delta_{0,0}
both in (
5.2.2
) and (
105
) arises from the commutator of
\gamma
operators,

\displaystyle\big[\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm\Lambda}}}}\tilde{\gamma}^{{\kern-9.06946pt{\rm\Lambda}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm\Lambda}}}}\tilde{\gamma}^{{\kern-9.06946pt{\rm\Lambda}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm\Lambda}}}}\tilde{\gamma}^{{\kern-8.09724pt{\rm\Lambda}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm\Lambda}}}}\tilde{\gamma}^{{\kern-8.09724pt{\rm\Lambda}\kern 5.55557pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{n}}}},\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}}}}\tilde{\gamma}^{{\kern-10.53195pt{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\kern 5.55557pt{\nu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{m}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}}}}\tilde{\gamma}^{{\kern-10.53195pt{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\kern 5.55557pt{\nu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{m}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}}}}\tilde{\gamma}^{{\kern-9.55974pt{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\kern 5.55557pt{\nu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{m}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}}}}\tilde{\gamma}^{{\kern-9.55974pt{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\kern 5.55557pt{\nu}}}_{{\kern-5.16669pt\kern 5.55557pt{m}}}}\big]=\big[\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm\Lambda}}}}\gamma^{{\kern-7.7144pt{\rm\Lambda}\kern 4.20052pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-3.81163pt\kern 4.20052pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm\Lambda}}}}\gamma^{{\kern-7.7144pt{\rm\Lambda}\kern 4.20052pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-3.81163pt\kern 4.20052pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm\Lambda}}}}\gamma^{{\kern-5.07854pt{\rm\Lambda}\kern 2.53687pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-2.14798pt\kern 2.53687pt{n}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm\Lambda}}}}\gamma^{{\kern-4.35373pt{\rm\Lambda}\kern 1.81206pt{\mu}}}_{{\kern-1.42317pt\kern 1.81206pt{n}}}},\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}}}}\gamma^{{\kern-9.1769pt{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\kern 4.20052pt{\nu}}}_{{\kern-3.81163pt\kern 4.20052pt{m}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}}}}\gamma^{{\kern-9.1769pt{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\kern 4.20052pt{\nu}}}_{{\kern-3.81163pt\kern 4.20052pt{m}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}}}}\gamma^{{\kern-6.54103pt{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\kern 2.53687pt{\nu}}}_{{\kern-2.14798pt\kern 2.53687pt{m}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}}}}\gamma^{{\kern-5.81622pt{\rm{\Lambda^{\prime}}}\kern 1.81206pt{\nu}}}_{{\kern-1.42317pt\kern 1.81206pt{m}}}}\big]=n\eta^{\mu\nu}\delta_{\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}}\delta_{n+m,0},\qquad\forall~\Lambda,\Lambda^{\prime}=\rm(F,P)

(106)

It is important to notice that the right-hand side of (
5.2.2
) and (
105
) precisely represents a spectrum of thermal radiation (i.e., Bose-Einstein distribution) with
\hbar=k_{\rm B}=1
and an absolute temperature,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{T}_{{{\rm U}}}}{{T}_{{{\rm U}}}}{{T}_{{{\rm U}}}}{{T}_{{{\rm U}}}}={a\over 2\pi}.

(107)

This phenomenon corresponds to the well-known timelike Unruh effect
Olson:2010jy
. Thus, an observer on the Kasner worldsheet evolving with the time-evolution parameter
a
will detect particles in the vacuum state of the Minkowski worldsheet at the Unruh temperature given by (
107
), while an observer on the Minkowski worldsheet will detect no particles in its own vacuum state.

5.2.3
Triggering Hagedorn physics through time-like Unruh temperature

It is well understood that the framework of tensionless strings is intimately connected to string theory at the Hagedorn temperature
T_{\rm H}
. Arguably,
T_{\rm H}
defines an extreme and absolute limiting temperature within the string theory framework, at which a novel phase transition occurs, introducing new degrees of freedom
Atick:1988si
. Beyond this point, the canonical partition function describing all single-particle states within the string framework diverges, and the strings effectively become tensionless
Pisarski:1982cn
;
Olesen:1985ej
. For a string system continuously evolving with temperature
T_{\rm S}
, the effective tension
\mathcal{T}_{\rm eff}
is related to the Hagedorn temperature
T_{\rm H}
via:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\rm eff}=\mathcal{T}\sqrt{1-\left({T_{\rm S}\over T_{\rm H}}\right)^{2}},

(108)

where
\mathcal{T}
is the intrinsic string tension as defined in (
8
). Clearly,
\mathcal{T}_{\rm eff}\approx\mathcal{T}
as
T_{\rm S}\to 0
, and
\mathcal{T}_{\rm eff}\to 0
as the system temperature approaches
T_{\rm S}\to T_{\rm H}
. In this context, the analysis of tensionless worldsheets in
sections

2.3
and
4
is found to be highly relevant and useful. More precisely, the tensionless vacuum state
\lvert 0_{c}\rangle
(which is entirely distinct from the tensile vacuum states
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
and
\lvert 0_{\rm K}\rangle
) is conjectured to be the worldsheet manifestation of the emergent long string as the system approaches the
T_{\rm H}
limit
Bagchi:2015nca
. This conjecture is supported by the observation that
\lvert 0_{c}\rangle
can be obtained as a squeezed or coherent state (
91
) of the tensile worldsheet theory, representing a highly energized state analogous to the string system near the Hagedorn temperature. The new degrees of freedom induced by the Hagedorn phase transition in string theory can be interpreted as excitations of the worldsheet vacuum
\lvert 0_{c}\rangle
through the action of its creation operators
\{\mathchoice{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{\tilde{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}},\mathchoice{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}{{c}^{{{\mu}}}_{{{-n}}}}\}
.

We now aim to explore how the timelike Unruh physics induced on the inertial worldsheet from the perspective of the Kasner observer is connected to Hagedorn physics. Specifically, we investigate how the timelike Unruh temperature
T_{\rm U}
(as derived in (
107
)) at the worldsheet tensionless limit triggers the same physics as that emerging from the Hagedorn phase transition at
T_{\rm S}\to T_{\rm H}
. In this analysis, we interpret the worldsheet evolving toward its tensionless point in terms of
\epsilon
-parameter flow approaching the
\epsilon\to 0
limit. Additionally, we found that the
\epsilon
-parameter flow can be manifested in terms of
a
-parameter evolution, which is mapped via:

\displaystyle\tanh\theta={\epsilon-1\over\epsilon+1}=-e^{-{\pi\Omega_{n}\over a}},

(109)

where
\tanh\theta
represents the conventional ratio of Bogoliubov coefficients from the transformations (
72
) and (
20
). From this stage, it is straightforward to translate everything into the language of a worldsheet evolving with increasing timelike Unruh temperature
T_{\rm U}
. In this process, we observe that the Minkowski worldsheet follows a thermodynamic relation analogous to (
108
) as seen by the Kasner worldsheet observer, yielding:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\rm eff}=\mathcal{T}\epsilon,\quad\epsilon=\tanh\left({\Omega_{n}\over 4T_{\rm U}}\right).

(110)

By comparing the typical effective tension forms (
108
) and (
110
), we identify

\displaystyle T_{\rm S}=T_{\rm H}\sqrt{1-\tanh^{2}\left({\Omega_{n}\over 4T_{\rm U}}\right)}.

(111)

Thus, the observer on the Kasner worldsheet will see that as the timelike Unruh temperature approaches infinity, the Minkowski worldsheet vacuum undergoes a transition from
\lvert 0_{\rm M}\rangle
to
\lvert 0_{c}\rangle
at
\epsilon\to 0
, mirroring the Hagedorn phase transition
T_{\rm S}\to T_{\rm H}
of tensionless strings, i.e.,

\displaystyle T_{\rm U}\to\infty\implies\epsilon\to 0,\quad T_{\rm S}\approx T_{\rm H}.

(112)

The above phenomenon of manifesting the Hagedorn phase transition on worldsheets suggests a different form of the
null string complementarity
discussed in
section

5.2.1
. This idea can be explained as follows. From the perspective of an observer on the Minkowski worldsheet, the tensionless vacuum
\lvert 0_{c}\rangle
is arguably experiencing Bose-Einstein condensation (BEC) as a consequence of the Hagedorn phase transition
Bagchi:2019cay
.
9
9

9

Readers may wonder how BEC, which typically occurs at extremely low (or absolute) temperatures, corresponds to the Hagedorn phase transition, a high-energy phenomenon. To clarify, note that for fundamental strings, the Hagedorn temperature is given by
T_{\rm H}={1\over 2\pi\sqrt{2\alpha^{\prime}}}
. Therefore, as the worldsheet approaches the Hagedorn phase transition by dialing the tensionless limit
\alpha^{\prime}\to\infty
, this triggers a very low value for
T_{\rm H}
, thereby connecting the worldsheet vacuum with BEC.
In contrast, an observer on the Kasner worldsheet would observe
\lvert 0_{c}\rangle
undergoing the same Hagedorn phase transition at a high temperature
T_{\rm U}\to\infty
, resulting in the worldsheet vacuum bubbling with high-energy particles uniformly spread across
\lvert 0_{c}\rangle
. This scenario is complementary to one where BEC formation is suppressed. This complementarity picture reflects the same ambiguity in interpreting the tensionless worldsheet vacuum as both a spacetime point (i.e., space-filling D-instanton) and an infinitely long string filling the entire spacetime (i.e., space-filling D-branes).
